\displaystyle I^{(k)}:=\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\gamma_{2}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}},\;\;II^{(k)}:={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\sum_{s=\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}+1}^{\gamma_{2}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{s}\boldsymbol{X}_{s}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{X}_{s}\in\mathcal{U}_{0}^{(k)}\}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}},

\displaystyle III^{(k)}:={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\sum_{s=\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}+1}^{\gamma_{2}}\boldsymbol{X}_{s}\boldsymbol{X}_{s}^{\prime}\left(\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}-\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{X}_{s}\in\mathcal{U}_{0}^{(k)}\}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}.

For the term
I^{(k)}
, due to (
42
),

\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}/\gamma_{2}\leq a^{*}/(2m_{X}^{2}s_{0})
, and thus,
by Assumption
4 (a)
,

I^{(k)}\leq\frac{2\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}m_{X}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}\leq\frac{a^{*}}{s_{0}},\;\;\text{ for }k\in[K].

(46)

Next, we consider the term
II^{(k)}
. Due to (
42
), we have

\gamma_{2}-\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}\geq\gamma_{2}/2\geq(16m_{X}^{4}/(a^{*})^{2})s_{0}^{5}\log(dT).

Thus, by Lemma
28
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{k\in[K]}\left\{II^{(k)}\leq\frac{a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right\}\right)\leq 1-\frac{2K}{T^{2}}.

(47)

Furthermore, we focus on the term
III^{(k)}
. For each
t\in[T]
and
k\in[K]
, define

\displaystyle Z_{t}^{(k)}:=|\mathbbm{1}\{A_{t}=k\}-\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}>\max_{\ell\neq k}((\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\}|\in\{0,1\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{t}^{(k)}=\bigcap_{\ell\neq k}\left\{|(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|>\frac{16m_{X}}{a^{*}}s_{0}\lambda_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}}\right\},

and further

\mathcal{A}=\bigcap_{m=r}^{R}\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\biggl\{|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\leq\frac{8m_{X}}{a^{*}}s_{0}\lambda_{m\gamma_{1}}\text{ for all }m\gamma_{1}<t\leq(m+1)\gamma_{1}\biggr\}.

On the event
\mathcal{A}
, for each
k\in[K]
,
m\in\{r,\cdots,R\}
and
t\in(m\gamma_{1},(m+1)\gamma_{1}]
, we have that for
\ell\in[K]
and
\ell\neq k
,

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(k)}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}-(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\leq\frac{16m_{X}}{a^{*}}s_{0}\lambda_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}},

which implies that on the event
\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{B}_{t}^{(k)}
,

\{A_{t}=k\}\quad\text{ if and only if}\quad\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}>\max_{\ell\neq k}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)}),

and thus

Z_{t}^{(k)}\mathbbm{1}\{\mathcal{A}\}\;=\;Z_{t}^{(k)}\mathbbm{1}\{\mathcal{A}\}\mathbbm{1}\{(\mathcal{B}_{t}^{(k)})^{c}\}\;\leq\;\mathbbm{1}\{(\mathcal{B}_{t}^{(k)})^{c}\}.

Therefore, due to Assumption
4 (a)
,

\displaystyle III^{(k)}\mathbbm{1}\{\mathcal{A}\}\leq m_{X}^{2}\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{\gamma_{2}}\mathbbm{1}\{(\mathcal{B}_{t}^{(k)})^{c}\}.

(48)

By Lemma
12
, for each
m=1,\cdots,R
, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}
, we have that

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{1}\leq\frac{8s_{0}\lambda_{m\gamma_{1}}}{a^{*}},\quad\text{ for each }k\in[K].

Since
|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\leq\|\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\|_{\infty}\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{1}
, due to Assumption
4 (a)
,

\displaystyle\bigcap_{m=r}^{R}\left(\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\right)\;\subseteq\;\mathcal{A},

which, in view of (
44
) and due to the union bound, implies that

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\geq 1-\sum_{m=r}^{R}\mathbb{P}\left((\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}})^{c}\right)\geq 1-\frac{6K}{T^{2}}.

Further, due to Assumption
5 (b)
,
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)}\|_{2}\geq L_{4}^{-1}
.
Due to (
37
) and the union bound, we have that for
t\in[T]
and
k\in[K]

\mathbb{P}((\mathcal{B}_{t}^{(k)})^{c})\leq K\times C^{*}L_{4}\frac{16m_{X}}{a^{*}}s_{0}\lambda_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}}.

By the definition of
\lambda_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}}
in (
29
) and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}
in (
43
),

\lambda_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}}\leq 6m_{X}/(C_{\gamma_{2}}^{1/4}s_{0}^{2})
. Then due to (
42
), we have

\mathbb{P}((\mathcal{B}_{t}^{(k)})^{c})\leq a^{*}/(2m_{X}^{2}s_{0})
for
t\in[T]
and
k\in[K]
. By Lemma
39
with
\delta=a^{*}/(2m_{X}^{2}s_{0})
, we have that for
k\in[K]
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{\gamma_{2}}\mathbbm{1}\{(\mathcal{B}_{t}^{(k)})^{c}\}\geq\frac{a^{*}}{m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{3a^{*}}{16m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}\gamma_{2}\right)\leq\frac{1}{T^{2}},

where the last inequality is due to (
42
). As a result, due to (
48
), since
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A})\geq 1-6K/T^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{k\in[K]}\left\{III^{(k)}\leq\frac{a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right\}\right)\geq 1-\frac{7K}{T^{2}}.

(49)

Finally, combining inequalities in (
46
), (
47
), and (
49
) on the three terms, in view of (
45
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{k\in[K]}\left\{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}\leq\frac{3a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right\}\right)\geq 1-\frac{9K}{T^{2}}.

The proof is complete.
∎

D.4
Proof of Theorem
11
, Part (ii): Stage 3

In this subsection, we focus on Stage 3 of the procedure defined in Definition
3
and establish part (ii) of Theorem
11
, which follows immediately from Lemma
23
.

Recall the definition of
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}
in (
25
) and
\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(k)}
in (
27
). Recall

\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)}
and
\boldsymbol{Y}_{[t]}^{(k)}
in (
26
), the Lasso and OPT-Lasso estimators
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)}
in Subsection
3.1
, and
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{(k)}_{t}
and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}
in (
28
).

Recall the fixed constants
C^{*}\geq 1
and
a^{*},h^{*},\ell_{0}^{*},\ell_{1}^{*}>0
in (
31
), which only depend only on
K,m_{X},\alpha_{X},L_{3},m_{\theta},L_{4}
, such that (
32
)-(
37
) hold.

Lemma 23
.

Suppose that Assumptions
4
and
5
hold. We set the regularization parameter
\lambda_{t},\lambda^{\textup{OPT}}_{t}
as in (
29
), and the end times
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
of Stage 1 and 2, respectively, as in (
30
).
If the integers
C_{\gamma_{1}}
and
C_{\gamma_{2}}
in (
30
) satisfy conditions (
42
) and (
50
).
Then, for each
m=1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/\gamma_{2}\rfloor
, there exists an event
\mathcal{A}_{m\gamma_{2}}\in\mathcal{F}_{m\gamma_{2}}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{m\gamma_{2}})\geq 1-17K/T
, and

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbbm{1}{\{\mathcal{A}_{m\gamma_{2}}\}}\right]\leq 7L_{3}^{2}\sum_{j\in S_{k}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}_{j})^{2}\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}_{j}|\leq 2\lambda^{\textup{OPT}}_{m\gamma_{2}}\right\}+32L_{3}^{2}m_{X}^{2}\sigma^{2}\frac{s_{0}}{m\gamma_{2}}.

Fix some
m\in\{1,\ldots,\lfloor T/\gamma_{2}\rfloor\}
, and denote by
t:=m\gamma_{2}
. Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{t}:=\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\left\{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{t}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\textup{max}}\leq\frac{3a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right\}\bigcap\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\left\{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda^{\textup{OPT}}_{t}\right\}.

By Lemma
24
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{t})\geq 1-17K/T
since
m\leq T
in that context.

Since
3a^{*}\leq 1/(2L_{3})
due to (
35
), by Lemma
38
and Assumption
4 (b)
, on the event
\mathcal{A}_{t}
, the following holds:

\frac{L_{3}^{-1}}{2}\leq\lambda_{\min}\left((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{t}^{(k)})_{S_{k},S_{k}}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{t}^{(k)})_{S_{k},S_{k}}\right)\leq\frac{3L_{3}}{2}\;\text{ for }k\in[K]

where the convention is that
\lambda_{\min}\left((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{t}^{(k)})_{S_{k},S_{k}}\right)=\lambda_{\max}\left((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{t}^{(k)})_{S_{k},S_{k}}\right)=1
if
S_{k}
is empty. As a result, due to Lemma
14
, on the event
\mathcal{A}_{t}
, for
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(6L_{3}^{2}+1\right)\sum_{j\in S_{k}}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}_{j})^{2}\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}_{j}|\leq 2\lambda^{\textup{OPT}}_{t}\right\}+8L_{3}^{2}\left\|\frac{(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}}{t}\right\|_{2}^{2},

where
(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}
is zero if
S_{k}
is empty. By Lemma
30
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{t}(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{4(m_{X}\sigma)^{2}s_{0}}{t}.

Then the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 24
.

Suppose that Assumptions
4
and
5
hold. We set the regularization parameter
\lambda_{t},\lambda^{\textup{OPT}}_{t}
as in (
29
), and the end time
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
of Stage 1 and 2, respectively, as in (
30
).
If the integers
C_{\gamma_{1}}
and
C_{\gamma_{2}}
in (
30
) satisfy condition (
42
) and

\displaystyle C_{\gamma_{2}}\geq\max\left\{\left(\frac{4000KL_{3}^{2}L_{4}m_{X}^{4}(C^{*})^{2}}{a^{*}}\right)^{2},\;\left(\frac{30L_{3}^{2}m_{X}C^{*}}{\ell_{1}^{*}}\right)^{2}\right\}.

(50)

then, for each
m=1,2,\ldots,\lfloor T/\gamma_{2}\rfloor
, with probability at least
1-17mK/T^{2}
, the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}
occurs, where

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}:=\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\left\{\boldsymbol{X}_{[m\gamma_{2}]}^{(k)}\textup{ satisfies }\textup{RE}(s_{0},3,a^{*})\right\},\\
&\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}:=\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\left\{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\textup{max}}\leq\frac{3a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right\},\\
&\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}=\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\left\{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{\infty}\leq\lambda^{\textup{OPT}}_{m\gamma_{2}}\right\}.\end{split}

(51)

For
k\in[K]
and
t\in[T]
, define

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{t}^{(k)}:=\{j\in[d]:|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)})_{j}|>\lambda^{\textup{OPT}}_{n}\},

which is the estimated support by thresholding the Lasso estimator; see Definition
2
.
For
m=1,2,\cdots,\lfloor T/\gamma_{2}\rfloor
, we define the following events: with
t=m\gamma_{2}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}:=\bigcap_{k\in[K]}\left\{\frac{\left\|({\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)}})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}\right\|_{\infty}}{t}\leq\frac{\lambda_{t}}{2},\;\;\frac{\left\|(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}\right\|_{2}}{t}\leq C^{*}m_{X}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{3s_{0}\log(dT)}{t}}\right\},\\
&\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{OPT}}:=\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\left\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{2}\leq\frac{a^{*}}{4KC^{*}L_{4}m_{X}^{3}s_{0}^{2}}\wedge\ell_{1}^{*},\;\;\;\tilde{S}_{t}^{(k)}\subseteq S_{k}\right\},\end{split}

(52)

where
(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}=0
if
S_{k}
is empty.

By Lemma
29
and
30
(see also (
36
)), due to the union bound, for
m=1,\ldots,\lfloor T/\gamma_{2}\rfloor
,

\mathbb{P}(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}})\geq 1-\frac{2K}{T^{4}}-\frac{K}{T^{2}}\geq 1-\frac{3K}{T^{2}}.

(53)

We proceed by induction. The base case is established in Lemma
22
. Now, assume that for some
1\leq m<\lfloor T/\gamma_{2}\rfloor
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\right)\geq 1-\frac{17mK}{T^{2}},

(54)

and we show that
\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{Lasso}}\right)\geq 1-{17(m+1)K}/{T^{2}}
.

Step 1:
we deal with the event

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{OPT}}
.

On the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, due to Lemma
38
and since
3a^{*}\leq 1/(2L_{3})
due to (
35
), we have that
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{eigen}}
holds, where

\ \tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{eigen}}:=\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\left\{\frac{L_{3}^{-1}}{2}\leq\lambda_{\min}\left((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)})_{S_{k},S_{k}}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)})_{S_{k},S_{k}}\right)\leq\frac{3L_{3}}{2}\right\},

and the convention is that
\lambda_{\min}\left((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)})_{S_{k},S_{k}}\right)=\lambda_{\max}\left((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)})_{S_{k},S_{k}}\right)=1
if
S_{k}
is empty.

By Lemma
13
, on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}\subseteq S_{k},\text{ for each }k\in[K].

Further, on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{eigen}}
, by Lemma
14
, for each
k\in[K]
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(6L_{3}^{2}+1\right)s_{0}\left(2\lambda^{\textup{OPT}}_{m\gamma_{2}}\right)^{2}+8L_{3}^{2}\left\|\frac{(\boldsymbol{X}_{[m\gamma_{2}]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[m\gamma_{2}]}}{m\gamma_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Due to the definition of
\lambda^{\textup{OPT}}_{t}
in (
29
), on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{eigen}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}
, for each
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 28L_{3}^{2}s_{0}(28L_{3})^{2}(6m_{X}\sigma)^{2}\frac{\log(dT)}{C_{\gamma_{2}}\sigma^{2}s_{0}^{5}\log(dT)}

\displaystyle+8L_{3}^{2}(C^{*}m_{X}\sigma)^{2}\frac{3s_{0}\log(dT)}{C_{\gamma_{2}}\sigma^{2}s_{0}^{5}\log(dT)},

which, due to (
50
), implies that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{2}\leq\frac{a^{*}}{4KC^{*}L_{4}m_{X}^{3}s_{0}^{2}}\wedge\ell_{1}^{*},\text{ for each }k\in[K].

Since
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{eigen}}
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{OPT}}.

(55)

Step 2:
we deal with the events

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{Lasso}}
.

Applying Lemma
12

with
\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}
, and due to Assumption
4 (c)
, on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}
, for each
k\in[K]
,

\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{\infty}\leq L_{3}\left(4+\frac{8s_{0}}{a^{*}}\times\frac{3a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right)\lambda_{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}=\lambda^{\textup{OPT}}_{m\gamma_{2}},

which implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\;\subseteq\;\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{Lasso}}.

(56)

Step 3:
we deal with the events

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}}
.

By an argument similar to that in the proof of Lemma
21
(see (
41
)), with
\gamma_{1}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)}
replaced by
\gamma_{2}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)}
, respectively, we have that on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}
, for each
k\in[K]
and
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{C}(s_{0},3)
,

\displaystyle\frac{\|\boldsymbol{X}_{[(m+1)\gamma_{2}]}^{(k)}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}\geq\frac{m}{m+1}a^{*}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{m+1}\left(\widetilde{I}_{m+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{v})-\sum_{i\in[K]}\widetilde{II}_{m+1}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{v})\right),

where we define

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\;\;\widetilde{I}_{m+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{v}):=\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\sum_{t=m\gamma_{2}+1}^{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\in\mathcal{U}_{h^{*}}^{(k)}\}\right)\boldsymbol{v},\\
&\widetilde{II}_{m+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{v}):=\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\sum_{t=m\gamma_{2}+1}^{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{|(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\geq h^{*}/2\}\right)\boldsymbol{v}.\end{aligned}

Further, we define the following two events

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m+1}:=\bigcap_{k\in[K]}\left\{\widetilde{I}_{m+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{v})\geq\ell_{0}^{*}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}\;\text{ for all }\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{C}(s_{0},3)\right\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{m+1}=\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\left\{\widetilde{II}_{m+1}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\frac{K-1}{K}a^{*}\|v\|_{2}^{2}\;\text{ for all }\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{C}(s_{0},3)\right\}.

Due to (
33
) and since
\gamma_{2}\geq\gamma_{1}
, we have that
\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m+1}\right)\geq 1-2K/T^{4}
. Further, on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{OPT}}
defined in (
52
), for each
k\in[K]
,
\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{2}\leq\ell_{1}^{*}
. Thus, since
\{\boldsymbol{X}_{t}:m\gamma_{2}<t\leq(m+1)\gamma_{2}\}
are independent from
\mathcal{F}_{m\gamma_{2}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{OPT}}\in\mathcal{F}_{m\gamma_{2}}
, due to (
34
) with
\boldsymbol{u}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}
, we have that almost surely,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{m+1}|\mathcal{F}_{m\gamma_{2}}\right)\mathbbm{1}\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{OPT}}\}\geq(1-2K/T^{4})\mathbbm{1}\{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{OPT}}\}.

By the definition of
a^{*}
in (
35
),
\ell_{0}^{*}\geq a^{*}K
. Thus, due to (
41
) and the definition
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}}
in (
39
),

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\bigcap\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m+1}\bigcap\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{m+1}\;\subseteq\;\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}},

(57)

which, due to (
55
) and the union bound, implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left((\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}})^{c}\;\cap\;(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}})\right)\\
\leq&\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m+1}^{c}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left((\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}_{m+1})^{c}\;\cap\;{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{OPT}}}\right)\leq 4K/T^{2}.\end{split}

(58)

Step 4:
we deal with the events

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
.

Recall the definition of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{t}^{(k)}
and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}
in (
28
), and
\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(k)}
in (
27
). By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{I}^{(k)}+\overline{II}^{(k)}+\overline{III}^{(k)},\;\;\text{ for }k\in[K],

(59)

where we define
\overline{I}^{(k)}:=\frac{m}{m+1}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}
, and

\displaystyle\overline{II}^{(k)}:=\frac{1}{m+1}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\sum_{s=m\gamma_{2}+1}^{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{s}\boldsymbol{X}_{s}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{X}_{s}\in\mathcal{U}_{0}^{(k)}\}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}},

\displaystyle\overline{III}^{(k)}:=\frac{1}{m+1}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\sum_{s=m\gamma_{2}+1}^{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}\boldsymbol{X}_{s}\boldsymbol{X}_{s}^{\prime}\left(\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}-\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{X}_{s}\in\mathcal{U}_{0}^{(k)}\}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}.

On the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
defined in (
51
), for each,
k\in[K]

\overline{I}^{(k)}:=\frac{m}{m+1}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}\leq\frac{m}{m+1}\frac{3a^{*}}{s_{0}},\quad\text{ for }k\in[K].

(60)

Next, we consider the term
\overline{II}^{(k)}
. Due to (
42
),
\gamma_{2}\geq(16m_{X}^{4}/(a^{*})^{2})s_{0}^{5}\log(dT)
. Thus, by Lemma
28
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{k\in[K]}\left\{\overline{II}^{(k)}\leq\frac{a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right\}\right)\geq 1-\frac{2K}{T^{2}}.

(61)

Furthermore, we focus on the term
\overline{III}^{(k)}
. For each
t\in[T]
and
k\in[K]
, define

\displaystyle Z_{t}^{(k)}:=|\mathbbm{1}\{A_{t}=k\}-\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}>\max_{\ell\neq k}((\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\}|\in\{0,1\},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{(k)}:=\bigcap_{\ell\neq k}\left\{|(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|>\frac{a^{*}}{2KC^{*}L_{4}m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}\right\},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}:=\bigcap_{k\in[K]}\bigcap_{t=m\gamma_{2}+1}^{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}\left\{|(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\leq\frac{a^{*}}{4KC^{*}L_{4}m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}\right\},

On the event
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}
, for
t\in(m\gamma_{2}+1,(m+1)\gamma_{2}]
and
k\in[K]
, we have

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(k)}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}-(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\leq\frac{a^{*}}{2KC^{*}L_{4}m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}.

Therefore on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{(k)}
,
\{A_{t}=k\}
if and only if

(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}>\max_{\ell\neq k}((\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}
, which implies that

Z_{t}^{(k)}\mathbbm{1}\{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}\}=Z_{t}^{(k)}\mathbbm{1}\{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}\}\mathbbm{1}\{(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{(k)})^{c}\}\leq\mathbbm{1}\{(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{(k)})^{c}\}.

(62)

Further, due to Assumption
5 (b)
,
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)}\|_{2}\geq L_{4}^{-1}
.
Due to (
37
) and the union bound, we have

\mathbb{P}((\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{(k)})^{c})\leq K\times C^{*}L_{4}\frac{a^{*}}{2KC^{*}L_{4}m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}=\frac{a^{*}}{2m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}.

By Lemma
39
with
\delta=a^{*}/(2m_{X}^{2}s_{0})
and due to (
42
), we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\sum_{s=m\gamma_{2}+1}^{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}\mathbbm{1}{\{(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{(k)})^{c}\}}\geq\frac{a^{*}}{m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{3a^{*}}{16m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}\gamma_{2}\right)\leq\frac{1}{T^{2}}.

Thus, due to (
62
) and Assumption
4 (a)
, for each
k\in[K]
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\overline{III}^{(k)}\mathbbm{1}\{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}\}\geq\frac{a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}}\sum_{s=m\gamma_{2}+1}^{(m+1)\gamma_{2}}\mathbbm{1}{\{(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{t}^{(k)})^{c}\}}\geq\frac{a^{*}}{m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{T^{2}}.

(63)

Finally, we consider the event
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}
.
For
k\in[K]
and
m\gamma_{2}<t\leq(m+1)\gamma_{2}
, due to Assumption
4 (a)
, on the event
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{OPT}}
defined in (
52
) (in particular,
\tilde{S}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}\subseteq S_{k}
), we have

\displaystyle|(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|=|(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t})_{S_{k}}|\leq\|(\boldsymbol{X}_{t})_{S_{k}}\|_{2}\|(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|(\boldsymbol{X}_{t})_{S_{k}}\|_{2}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{2}}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{s_{0}}m_{X}\frac{a^{*}}{4KC^{*}L_{4}m_{X}^{3}s_{0}^{2}}\leq\frac{a^{*}}{4KC^{*}L_{4}m_{X}^{2}s_{0}}.

Thus,
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{OPT}}\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{A}}
. Due to (
55
), we have

\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{A}}

Due to (
60
), (
61
) and (
63
). in view of (
59
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left((\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})^{c}\;\cap\;(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}})\right)\\
\leq&\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{k\in[K]}\left\{\overline{II}^{(k)}\geq\frac{a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right\}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{k\in[K]}\left\{\overline{III}^{(k)}\mathbbm{1}\{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}\}\geq\frac{a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right\}\right)\leq\frac{3K}{T^{2}}.\end{split}

(64)

Final step:
combining the above steps.

Due to (
56
), we have

\displaystyle(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{Lasso}})^{c}\;\subseteq\;(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}})^{c}\;\cup\;(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})^{c}\;\cup\;(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}})^{c}.

By the union bound,
\mathbb{P}\left((\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{Lasso}})^{c}\right)
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left((\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}})^{c}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left((\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}})^{c}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left((\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}})^{c}\right)+

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left((\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}})^{c}\;\cap\;(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}})\right)+\mathbb{P}\left((\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})^{c}\;\cap\;(\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}})\right).

By the inductive hypothesis in (
54
), and inequalities in (
53
), (
58
) and (
64
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left((\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m+1}^{\boldsymbol{\theta}})^{c}\right)\leq\frac{17mK}{T^{2}}+\frac{6K}{T^{2}}+\frac{4K}{T^{2}}+\frac{3K}{T^{2}}\leq\frac{17(m+1)K}{T^{2}},

which finishes the inductive step, and the proof is complete.
∎

D.5
Supporting lemmas

Recall that for each
k\in[K]
,
S_{k}
is the support of
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}
, and
s_{0}=\max_{k\in[K]}|S_{k}|\geq 1
. Further, recall the notations in Subsection
3.2
, and the definition of
\mathcal{C}(s,\kappa)
in Definition
4
.
The following lemmas either do not depend on the arm selection mechanism or hold for any such mechanism.

Lemma 25
.

Let
\{B_{t},t\in[n]\}
be a sequence of i.i.d. Bernoulli random variables, independent from any other random variables, with
\mathbb{E}[B_{1}]=1/K
.
Suppose that Assumptions
4 (a)
and
4 (b)
hold. Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
\alpha_{X},K,L_{3}
, such that for any
n\geq Cs_{0}\log(dT)
, with probability at least
1-2/T^{4}
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}B_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\geq\frac{1}{4KL_{3}}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2},\quad\text{ for all }\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{C}(s_{0},3).

Due to Assumption
4 (a)
, for any
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}=1
, we have
\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}B_{t}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\alpha_{X}
for
t\in[n]
. Further, due to independence,
we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}B_{t}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}]\mathbb{E}[B_{t}]=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}/K,

which, due to Assumption
4 (b)
, implies that

\frac{1}{KL_{3}}\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}B_{t}]\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}B_{t}]\right)\leq\frac{L_{3}}{K}

Applying
Lemma
42
to the random matrix
(B_{1}\boldsymbol{X}_{1},\ldots,B_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})^{\prime}
, the proof is complete.
∎

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(k)}
in (
27
).

Lemma 26
.

Suppose that Assumptions
4 (a)
,
4 (b)
and
5
hold. Then there exist constants
h,\ell_{0}>0
depending only on
K,L_{3},m_{\theta},L_{4}
and
C>0
depending only on
\alpha_{X},K,L_{3},m_{\theta},L_{4}
, such that for any
n\geq Cs_{0}\log d
, with probability at least
1-2Ke^{-n/C}
, we have

\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(k)}\}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\geq\ell_{0}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2},\quad\text{ for all }\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{C}(s_{0},3)\text{ and }k\in[K].

By Lemma
37
and due to Assumption
4 (b)
, there exist constants
h,\ell_{0}>0
, which depend only on
K,L_{3},m_{\theta},L_{4}
, such that for
t\in[T]
and
k\in[K]
,

\ell_{0}\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(k)}\}\right]\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(k)}\}\right]\right)\leq L_{3}.

The proof is then complete by applying Lemma
42
to
the random matrix
(\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(k)}\},\ldots,\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(k)}\})^{\prime}
for each
k\in[K]
and the union bound.
∎

Lemma 27
.

Suppose that Assumptions
4 (a)
,
4 (b)
and
5
hold. Then for any constants
h,\ell_{2}>0
, there exist a constant
\ell_{1}>0
depending only on
h,\ell_{2},L_{3}
and a constant
C>0
depending only on
\ell_{2},\alpha_{X},L_{3}
, such that for all
n\geq Cs_{0}\log d
and
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq\ell_{1}
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-n/C}
, we have

\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\geq h/2\}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\leq\ell_{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2},\quad\text{ for all }\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{C}(s_{0},3).

Let
\tilde{\ell}_{2}>0
be the unique real number such that
\ell_{2}=(9/4)[\tilde{\ell}_{2}+3(\tilde{\ell}_{2})^{2}]
, and define
\tau:=\tilde{\ell}_{2}/\sqrt{L_{3}}
. For
t\in[n]
and
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, define
\boldsymbol{Z}_{t,\boldsymbol{u}}:=\boldsymbol{X}_{t}(\mathbbm{1}\{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\geq h/2\}+\tau)
. Then

\boldsymbol{Z}_{t,\boldsymbol{u}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{t,\boldsymbol{u}}^{\prime}=(1+2\tau)\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\geq h/2\}+\tau^{2}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}.

(65)

By Assumption
4 (b)
we have

\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{Z}_{t,\boldsymbol{u}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{t,\boldsymbol{u}}^{\prime}]\right)\geq\tau^{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}]\right)\geq\tau^{2}L_{3}^{-1}.

By Lemma
36
, there exists a constant
\ell_{1}>0
depending only on
\ell_{2},h,L_{3}
such that

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\geq h/2\right\}]\right)\leq\tilde{\ell}_{2},\;\text{ for all }\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ such that }\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq\ell_{1},

which, together with Assumption
4 (b)
, implies that for
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ such that }\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq\ell_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{Z}_{t,\boldsymbol{u}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{t,\boldsymbol{u}}^{\prime}]\right)\leq(1+2\tau)\tilde{\ell}_{2}+\tau^{2}L_{3}\leq(4/9)\ell_{2}.

Due to Assumption
4 (a)
,

\|\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z}_{t,\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq(1+\tau)\alpha_{X}
for any
\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\|\boldsymbol{\omega}\|_{2}=1
. Then, by Lemma
42
, there exists some constant
C>0
, depending only on
\alpha_{X},\ell_{2},L_{3}
, such that for all
n\geq Cs_{0}\log d
and
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\leq\ell_{1}
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-n/C}
, we have

\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{Z}_{t,\boldsymbol{u}}\boldsymbol{Z}_{t,\boldsymbol{u}}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\leq\frac{9}{4}\frac{4\ell_{2}}{9}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}=\ell_{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2},\;\;\text{ for any }\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{C}(s_{0},3).

The proof is then finished in view of (
65
).
∎

Recall the definition of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}
in (
28
).

Lemma 28
.

Let
\tau>0
be arbitrary. Suppose Assumption
4 (a)
holds. If

n\geq\frac{16m_{X}^{4}}{\tau^{2}}s_{0}^{2}\log(dT),

then with probability at least
1-2K/T^{2}
, for each
k\in[K]
,

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}>\max_{\ell\neq k}((\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}\leq\frac{\tau}{s_{0}}.

Note that by Assumption
4 (a)
we have

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}>\max_{\ell\neq k}((\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}\leq 2m_{X}^{2}.

Therefore, by Lemma
40
, if
n\geq(16m_{X}^{4}/\tau^{2})s_{0}^{2}\log(dT)
, then

\mathbb{P}\left({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}>\max_{\ell\neq k}((\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)})\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}\geq\frac{\tau}{s_{0}}\right)\leq\frac{2}{T^{2}}.

Then the proof is complete by the union bound.
∎

Recall the definition of
\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)}
in (
26
) and
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}
in (
25
). Recall that we say a random variable
Z
is
\alpha
-sub-Gaussian if
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\tau Z)]\leq\exp(\alpha^{2}\tau^{2}/2)
for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
, and that
\epsilon_{1}
is
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian.

Lemma 29
.

Suppose that
\|\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{X}
and let
t\in[T]
. Then for each arm
k\in[K]
and any
\delta>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\|({\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)}})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}/t\|_{\infty}\geq\delta\right)\leq 2d\exp\left\{-\frac{t\delta^{2}}{2(m_{X}\sigma)^{2}}\right\}.

Consequently,

\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{k\in[K]}\left\{\|{(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)}})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}/t\|_{\infty}\leq 3m_{X}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\log(dT)}{t}}\right\}\right)\geq 1-\frac{2K}{T^{4}}.

Fix
k\in[K]
,
j\in[d]
, and
t\in[T]
. By definition,

(({\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)}})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]})_{j}=\sum_{s=1}^{t}(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{j}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}\epsilon_{s}.

Clearly,
(({\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)}})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]})_{j}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
. Further, for
s\in[1,t]
, since
\epsilon_{s}
is independent from
\boldsymbol{X}_{s}
and
\mathcal{F}_{s-1}
, and
\epsilon_{s}
is
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian, we have that, for any
\tau>0
, almost surely,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{s}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}\cup\{\boldsymbol{X}_{s}\}\right]=0.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\tau(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{j}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}\epsilon_{s}\right)\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}\cup\{\boldsymbol{X}_{s}\}\right]\leq\exp\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left(\tau(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{j}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}\right)^{2}\sigma^{2}\right\}.

Thus,
\{(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{j}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}\epsilon_{s}:1\leq s\leq t\}
is an
\{\mathcal{F}_{s}\}
-martingale difference sequence. Further, since
\|\boldsymbol{X}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{X}
, we have that for
\tau>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\tau(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{j}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}\epsilon_{s}\right)\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}\right]\leq e^{\tau^{2}m_{X}^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}.

Finally, by Theorem 2.19 in
[
45
]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|(({\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)}})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]})_{j}|\geq t\delta\right)\leq 2\exp\left\{-\frac{t\delta^{2}}{2(m_{X}\sigma)^{2}}\right\}.

Then the first claim follows due to the union bound. The second claim follows immediately from the first claim and the union bound.
∎

Lemma 30
.

Suppose that
\|\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{X}
and let
t\in[T]
. Fix
k\in[K]
, and assume
S_{k}
is non-empty. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}/t\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq 4(m_{X}\sigma)^{2}s_{0}/t.

Further, there exists an absolute constant
C>0
, such that for any
\tau>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}/t\|_{2}\geq Cm_{X}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{s_{0}\log(s_{0})+s_{0}\tau}{t}}\right)\leq 2e^{-\tau}.

In particular, if
S_{k}
is non-empty for all
k\in[K]
, then

\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{k=1}^{K}\left\{\|(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}/t\|_{2}\leq Cm_{X}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{3s_{0}\log(dT)}{t}}\right\}\right)\geq 1-\frac{K}{T^{2}}.

Note that

(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}=\sum_{s=1}^{t}(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{S_{k}}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}\epsilon_{s}
.

Step 1. Expectation bound.
Applying the same reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
29
, we have that the sequence
\{(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{S_{k}}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}\epsilon_{s}:s\in[t]\}
forms a vector-valued
\{\mathcal{F}_{s}\}
-martingale difference sequence. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{1}^{2}]\sum_{s\in[t]}\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{S_{k}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{S_{k}}].

Since
\epsilon_{1}
is
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian, we have
\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{1}^{2}]\leq 4\sigma^{2}
.
The proof then is complete since
\|\boldsymbol{X}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{X}
.

Step 2. Probability bounds.
Applying the same reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
29
, we have that the sequence
\{(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{S_{k}}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}\epsilon_{s}:s\in[t]\}
forms a vector-valued
\{\mathcal{F}_{s}\}
-martingale difference sequence, and that
for any
\tau>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|(\boldsymbol{X}_{s})_{S_{k}}\mathbbm{1}\{A_{s}=k\}\epsilon_{s}\|_{2}\geq\tau\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1})\leq 2\exp\left\{-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2(m_{X}\sigma\sqrt{s_{0}})^{2}}\right\}.

Then, the first probability bound follows immediately from Corollary 7 in
[
19
]
with
\delta=2e^{-\tau}
.
The second probability bound is finished by setting
\tau=2\log(dT)
and the union bound.
∎

E
Proofs for the Bandit Problem - Lower Bounds

E.1
Proof of Theorem
7

In this subsection, we prove Theorem
7
, establishing a lower bound on the cumulative regret over all permissible rules for the case
K=2
.

For technical analysis, we consider the following equivalent problem formulation. For
t\in[T]
, define

\displaystyle Y_{t}^{(k)}=\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}+\epsilon_{t}^{(k)},

(66)

where
\{\epsilon_{t}^{(k)}:t\in[T],k\in[2]\}
are i.i.d. with the normal distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
. We may view
Y_{t}^{(1)}
and
Y_{t}^{(2)}
as the potential rewards, and when an arm
A_{t}\in[K]
is selected, then
Y_{t}=Y_{t}^{(A_{t})}
is observed.

Compared to the formulation in Section
3
, the model in (
66
) assigns independent noise to each arm. However, since only one arm is pulled at each time, the two formulations are equivalent: for any admissible policy
\{\pi_{t}:t\in[T]\}
as defined in Section
3
, the joint distribution of the observations
\{(\boldsymbol{X}_{t},\boldsymbol{Y}_{t},A_{t}):t\in[T]\}
, as well as the regret sequence
\{r_{t}:t\in[T]\}
, are identical under both formulations.

Recall the two families of distributions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
:
\omega_{1}(\cdot)
in Definition
5
and
\omega_{2}(\cdot)
in Definition
6
, and the definition of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{d}(s_{0},L)
prior to Theorem
7
. The proof strategy follows a similar approach to that in Section 6 of
[
36
]
, but with different prior distributions.

Recall that in Theorem
7
, we assume that for some constant
\kappa>1
, we have

3\leq s_{0}\leq(d+2)/3,\quad d\geq s_{0}^{\kappa},\quad T\geq\left(2(C_{*}\vee\sigma^{2}\vee 1)s_{0}^{5}\log(dT)\right)^{\kappa}.

(67)

In this proof, we consider the equivalent formulation given in (
66
). Fix any permissible rule and denote its arm selections by
\{A_{t}\in[2]:t\in[T]\}
and its instantaneous regrets by
\{r_{t}:t\in[T]\}
.

Step 1. Lower bound the supremum risk by the average risk.

Let
\tilde{\omega}_{1}
and
\tilde{\omega}_{2}
be two distributions on
\mathbb{R}^{d+d}
, both supported on
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{d}(s_{0},L)
, defined as follows.

i)

If a random vector

(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d}
has the distribution given by
\tilde{\omega}_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)}=\boldsymbol{0}_{d},\quad\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\text{ has the distribution }\omega_{1}(s_{0},2/L)\text{ in Definition }\ref{def: omega_1}.

(68)

By Definition
5
, under
\tilde{\omega}_{1}
, almost surely (a.s.),
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\|_{2}\geq 1/L
and
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\|_{2}\leq 2/L\leq 2L
.
Thus,
\tilde{\omega}_{1}
is supported on
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{d}(s_{0},L)
, which is defined prior to Theorem
7
.

ii)

If a random vector
(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})\in\mathbb{R}^{d+d}
has the distribution given by
\tilde{\omega}_{2}
, then

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)}=\boldsymbol{0}_{d},\quad\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\text{ has the distribution }\omega_{2}(s_{0},1/L,\delta_{*})\text{ in Definition }\ref{def: omega_2},

(69)

where we define

\displaystyle\delta_{*}:=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{16L_{3}T}\frac{s_{0}-1}{2}\log\left(\frac{d-s_{0}}{(s_{0}-1)/2}\right).

Due to Lemma
16
, under
\tilde{\omega}_{2}
, almost surely (a.s.),
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\|_{2}\geq 1/L
and
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{1/L^{2}+2\delta_{*}^{2}}\leq 2L
, where the last inequality is because we have
2\delta_{*}^{2}\leq 3L^{2}
due to (
67
). Thus,
\tilde{\omega}_{2}
is supported
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{d}(s_{0},L)
.

Now, let
\tilde{\omega}
be the mixture of
\tilde{\omega}_{1}
and
\tilde{\omega}_{2}
with equal weights. This mixture is also supported on
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{d}(s_{0},L)
. It is important to note that
\tilde{\omega}
does not depend on
t
. Then,

\displaystyle 2\sup_{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})\in\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{d}(s_{0},L)}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}}\left[\sum_{t=\tilde{\gamma}}^{T}r_{t}\right]\geq 2\mathbb{E}_{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})\sim\tilde{\omega}}\left[\sum_{t=\tilde{\gamma}}^{T}r_{t}\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})\sim\tilde{\omega}_{1}}\left[\sum_{t=\tilde{\gamma}}^{T}r_{t}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})\sim\tilde{\omega}_{2}}\left[\sum_{t=\tilde{\gamma}}^{T}r_{t}\right],

where
\mathbb{E}_{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})\sim\tilde{\omega}}
means that the parameters
(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})
is treated as a random vector with the prior distribution
\tilde{\omega}
, and for simplicity, we write it as
\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\omega}}
below."

Step 2. Lower bound expected regrets by
\ell_{2}
-estimation error.
Let
(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})
be a random vector
with the prior distribution
\tilde{\omega}
. Note that a.s.,
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)}=\boldsymbol{0}_{d}
. Then, for
t\in[T]
, since
r_{t}=\max_{k\in[2]}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}^{\prime}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(A_{t})}\right)
, a.s.,

\displaystyle r_{t}=(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}>0,\;A_{t}=1\}-(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}<0,\;A_{t}=2\}.

Recall that
\mathcal{F}_{t}=\sigma(\{(\boldsymbol{X}_{s},A_{s},Y_{s}),s\leq t\})
is the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the observations up to time
t
. Then, the optimal decision rule
A_{t}^{*}
, which minimizes the expected regret
\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\omega}}[r_{t}]
, can be achieved by the following:
A_{t}^{*}=1
if and only if

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\geq 0\}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\cup\boldsymbol{X}_{t}]

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}<0\}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\cup\boldsymbol{X}_{t}],

or equivalently,
A_{t}^{*}=1
if and only if
\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\cup\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\right]^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\leq 0
. Since
\boldsymbol{X}_{t}
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}
are independent, we have

\displaystyle A_{t}^{*}=\begin{cases}1,\;&\text{ if }(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\leq 0\\
2,\;&\text{ otherwise }\\
\end{cases},\quad\text{ where }\;\;\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right].

Thus, for any fixed rule
\{A_{t}:t\in[T]\}
, by the law of total expectation we have for
t\in[T]

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\omega}}[r_{t}]&\geq\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\omega}}[(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}>0\;,\;(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\leq 0\}\\
&\;\;\qquad-(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\leq 0\;,\;(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}>0\}]\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\omega}}\left[|(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\mathbbm{1}\{\textup{sgn}((\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\neq\textup{sgn}((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\}\right],\end{split}

(70)

where for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
, we define
\text{sgn}(\tau):=\mathbbm{1}\{\tau>0\}-\mathbbm{1}\{\tau<0\}
.

For
t\in[T]
, applying Lemma
33
to
\boldsymbol{X}_{t}
conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
, and due to Assumptions
4 (a)
and
4 (b)
, we have that there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
L_{3}
, such that, a.s.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\omega}}\left[|(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}|\mathbbm{1}\{\textup{sgn}((\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\neq\textup{sgn}((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\}\;|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]\geq

\displaystyle C^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\|_{2}\left\|\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}}{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\|_{2}}-\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)}}{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)}\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{CL}\left\|\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}}{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\|_{2}}-\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)}}{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)}\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2},

where we use the fact that
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\|_{2}\geq 1/L
a.s. under
\tilde{\omega}
and that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)}/\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)}\|_{2}
is interpreted as zero if the denominator is zero .

Step 3. Lower bound the
\ell_{2}
estimation errors.

For
t\in[T]
, we denote by

\mathcal{H}_{t}:=\sigma(\{(\boldsymbol{X}_{s},Y_{s}^{(1)},Y_{s}^{(2)}:s\in[t]\})

all potential observations up to time
t
. Note that
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\subseteq{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}
, and thus

\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)}/\|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{t-1}^{(2)}\|_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\subset{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}
, which, in view of (
70
), implies that

\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\omega}}\left[r_{t}\right]\geq\frac{1}{CL}\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{t-1}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\omega}}\left(\left\|\hat{\psi}_{t-1}-\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}}{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right),

where the infimum is taken over all
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued,
\mathcal{H}_{t-1}
-measurable random vectors.
Further, for
t\in[T]
, since
Y_{s}^{(1)}=\epsilon_{s}^{(1)}
for
s\in[t]
, we have
\{Y_{s}^{(1)}:s\in[t]\}
are independent from
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{t}:=\sigma(\{(\boldsymbol{X}_{s},Y_{s}^{(2)}:s\in[t]\})
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}
. Thus, the above infimum can be taken
over all
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued,
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}
-measurable random vectors.

Finally, by Lemma
15
and Lemma
17
, for some constant
C>0
, depending only on
L
and
L_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{\hat{\psi}_{t-1}\in{\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}_{t-1}}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\omega}}\left(\left\|\hat{\psi}_{t-1}-\frac{\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}}{\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)\geq C^{-1}\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}s_{0}}{t+\sigma^{2}s_{0}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{T}s_{0}\log\frac{d}{s_{0}}\right),

which implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\omega}}\left[\sum_{t=\tilde{\gamma}}^{T}r_{t}\right]\geq C^{-1}\sigma^{2}s_{0}\left(\log\left(\frac{T+\sigma^{2}s_{0}}{\tilde{\gamma}+1+\sigma^{2}s_{0}}\right)+\frac{T-\tilde{\gamma}}{T}\log\left(\frac{d}{s_{0}}\right)\right).

The proof is then complete due to (
67
).
∎

E.2
Proof of Theorem
10

In Theorem
10
,
K=2
, and for
(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})\in\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{d}(s_{0},L)
, Assumption
5
holds with
m_{\theta}=2L
and
L_{4}=L
. Further, we consider the specific case

\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(1)}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(2)}=2^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{d\times d},

where
\mathbb{I}_{d\times d}
is the
d
-by-
d
identity matrix. Then,
Assumptions
4 (b)
and
4 (c)
hold with
L_{3}=2
.

Recall the fixed constants
C^{*}\geq 1
and
a^{*},h^{*},\ell_{0}^{*},\ell_{1}^{*}>0
in (
31
), which only depend only on
K=2,m_{X},\alpha_{X},L_{3}=2,m_{\theta}=2L,L_{4}=L
, such that (
32
)-(
37
) hold.

Lemma 31
.

Consider the case
K=2
and set
\gamma_{2}=T
in Definition
3
, that is, without Stage 3.
Suppose that Assumption
4 (a)
holds, that
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(1)}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(2)}=2^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{d\times d}
, and that
(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})\in\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{d}(s_{0},L)
for some
L>1
and the supports
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are both non-empty. Set the regularization parameter
\lambda_{t}
and the end time
\gamma_{1}
of Stage 1
as follows:

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}=C_{\gamma_{1}}\lceil(\sigma^{2}\vee 1)\rceil s_{0}\lceil\log(dT)\rceil,\quad\lambda_{t}=48m_{X}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\log(dT)}{t}}.

(71)

Suppose that the integer
C_{\gamma_{1}}
in (
71
) satisfies the following:

\displaystyle C_{\gamma_{1}}\geq\max\left\{C^{*},\;\left(\frac{18m_{X}}{\ell_{1}^{*}a^{*}}\right)^{2},2\right\}.

(72)

Let
M_{*}
be the smallest integer satisfying:

\displaystyle M_{*}\geq\max\left\{4,\frac{32m_{X}^{4}}{(a^{*})^{2}},\left(\frac{3072LC^{*}m_{X}^{4}}{(a^{*})^{2}}\right)^{4}\right\}.

(73)

Then, for
m=M_{*}s_{0}^{4},\ldots,\lfloor T/\gamma_{1}\rfloor
, with probability at least
1-38/T^{2}
, the event
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Inc}}
occurs, where, denoting
t=m\gamma_{1}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}:=\bigcap_{k=1}^{2}\left\{\boldsymbol{X}_{[t]}^{(k)}\textup{ satisfies }\textup{RE}(s_{0},3,a^{*})\right\},\\
&\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}:=\bigcap_{k=1}^{2}\left\{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{t}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(k)}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\textup{max}}\leq\frac{3a^{*}}{s_{0}}\right\},\\
&\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}=\bigcap_{k=1}^{2}\left\{\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)}\|_{\infty}\leq 56\lambda_{t}\right\},\\
&\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Inc}}=\bigcap_{k=1}^{2}\left\{\hat{S}_{t}^{(k)}\;\subseteq\;S_{k}\right\},\end{split}

(74)

and
\hat{S}_{t}^{(k)}:=\{j\in[d]:(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)})_{j}\neq 0\}
is the support of the Lasso estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)}

By the same argument as in Lemma
22
, but with a slight different choice of
\lambda_{t}
, we have that for each
m=M_{*}s_{0}^{4},\ldots,\lfloor T/\gamma_{1}\rfloor
, with probability at least
1-34/T^{2}
, we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}
holds. As a result, due to the union bound, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left((\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Inc}})^{c}\;\cap\;\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right)\leq 4/T^{2}.

Fix
m\in\{M_{*}s_{0}^{4},\ldots,\lfloor T/\gamma_{1}\rfloor\}
, and denote by
t=m\gamma_{1}
. Since
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(1)}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(2)}=2^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{d\times d}=2^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
, Assumptions
4 (b)
and
4 (c)
hold with
L_{3}=2
. Thus, on the event
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, due to Lemma
38
and the definition of
a^{*}
in (
35
), for
k\in[2]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\leq\lambda_{\min}\left((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{t}^{(k)})_{S_{k},S_{k}}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left((\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{t}^{(k)})_{S_{k},S_{k}}\right)\leq 3,

(75)

which verifies condition (7.43a) in
[
45
]
.

Thus, for
k\in[2]
and
j\not\in S_{k}
, on the event
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left((\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)}\right)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],j}^{(k)}\right\|_{2}\leq 4\left\|t^{-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],j}^{(k)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\sqrt{s_{0}\times((3a^{*})/s_{0})^{2}}=12a^{*}/\sqrt{s_{0}}.

Due to (
35
),
a^{*}\leq 1/16
. By Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, for
k\in[2]
, on the event
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
,

\displaystyle\max_{j\not\in S_{k}}\left\|\left((\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)}\right)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],j}^{(k)}\right\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{s_{0}}\times 12a^{*}/\sqrt{s_{0}}\leq 3/4,

(76)

which verifies condition (7.43b) in
[
45
]
, and also implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max_{j\not\in S_{k}}\left|(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],j}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)}\left((\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)}\right)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}/t\right|\\
\leq&3/4\times\|(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}/t\|_{\infty}.\end{split}

(77)

Define
\mathcal{A}
to be the following event:

\displaystyle\bigcap_{k\in[2]}

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{j\not\in S_{k}}\left|(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],j}^{(k)})^{\prime}\left(\mathbb{I}_{|S_{k}|\times|S_{k}|}-\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)}\left((\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)}\right)^{-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],S_{k}}^{(k)})^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}/t\right|\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\leq 6m_{X}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\log(dT)}{t}}=\lambda_{t}/8\right\},

where we recall the choice of
\lambda_{t}
in (
71
). Note that on the event
\mathcal{A}
, condition (7.44) in
[
45
]
holds. Further, by Lemma
29
, in view of (
77
), we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{A}^{c}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right)\leq{4}/{T^{4}}.

By Theorem 7.21 in
[
45
]
, when events (
75
), (
76
), and
\mathcal{A}
all hold, we have
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Inc}}
holds. Thus,

\mathbb{P}\left((\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Inc}})^{c}\;\cap\;\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{A}^{c}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\right)\leq 4/T^{2}
. The proof is then complete.
∎

Define the following function: for
z_{1},z_{2}\in(-1/12,1/12)
,

\displaystyle f(z_{1},z_{2})=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+(-1/12)^{2}}}\begin{bmatrix}1\\
-\frac{1}{12}\end{bmatrix}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1-z_{1})^{2}+(-1/12+z_{2})^{2}}}\begin{bmatrix}1-z_{1}\\
-\frac{1}{12}+z_{2}\end{bmatrix}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

(78)

Lemma 32
.

There exists some absolute constant
c_{*}>0
such that for any
0<\delta<c_{*}
,

\displaystyle\inf_{(z_{1},z_{2})\in[\delta/2,5\delta]^{2}}\;f(z_{1},z_{2})\geq c_{*}\delta^{2}.

By elementary calculation, the gradient vector of
f
at
(0,0)
is the zero vector.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}=(1,1/12)^{\prime}
and
\boldsymbol{v}=(-1/12,1)^{\prime}
. Again by elementary calculation,
\boldsymbol{u}
and
\boldsymbol{v}
are the eigen-vectors of the Hessian matrix
\nabla^{2}f(0,0)
corresponding to eigen-values
0
and
2/(1+(1/12)^{2})
, respectively.
For
(z_{1},z_{2})\in[\delta/2,5\delta]^{2}
, we have

(z_{1},z_{2})\boldsymbol{v}\geq-\frac{5\delta}{12}+\frac{\delta}{2}=\frac{\delta}{12}>0.

Then the proof is complete by Taylor’s theorem.
∎

In the following proof, denote by
c_{*}
a constant such that Lemma
32
holds.

In this proof, we fix the following pair of arm parameters:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)}:=(1,\boldsymbol{0}_{d-1}^{\prime})^{\prime}\text{ with }S_{1}=\{1\},\quad\text{ and }\quad\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}:=(0,1/12,\boldsymbol{0}_{d-2}^{\prime})^{\prime},\text{ with }S_{2}=\{2\},

where
\boldsymbol{0}_{\ell}
is the all-zero vector with length
\ell
. Clearly,
(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)})\in\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{d}(s_{0},L)
for any
s_{0}\geq 1
and
L\geq 1
.

Recall that
M_{*}
is defined in (
73
) and that
\gamma_{1}=C_{\gamma_{1}}\lceil(\sigma^{2}\vee 1)\rceil s_{0}\lceil\log(dT)\rceil
in (
71
). We assume that
T\geq M_{*}s_{0}^{4}\gamma_{1}
and that the integer
C_{\gamma_{1}}
satisfies (
72
).

Step 1.
Control the estimation error.
In this step,
let
m\in\{M_{*}s_{0}^{4},\ldots,\lfloor T/\gamma_{1}\rfloor\}
.
By lemma
31
, since
T\geq 64
, with probability at least
0.99
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Inc}}
holds.
Further, applying Lemma
29
to the first two covariates, we have
\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}\right)\geq 0.41
, where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}:=\bigcap_{j\in[2]}\left\{\left|(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],j})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}/t\right|\leq\frac{2m_{X}\sigma}{\sqrt{t}}\right\},\quad\text{ and }t=m\gamma_{1}.

Thus, by the union bound, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{all}}\right)\geq 0.4,\quad\text{ where }\;\;\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{all}}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{RE}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Inc}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}.

(79)

Due to the definition of
M_{*}
in (
73
) and since
m\geq M_{*}s_{0}^{4}
, we have
56\lambda_{m\gamma_{1}}\leq 1/24
.
By a similar argument as in the proof of Theorem
5
, on the event
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Lasso}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{Inc}}
, we must have

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(1)}=S_{1}=\{1\},\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{S}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(2)}=S_{2}=\{2\},

(80)

and further for
k\in[2]
,

\displaystyle\frac{(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],k}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],k}^{(k)}}{t}[(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t}^{(k)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})_{k}]=\frac{(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],k}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}}{t}-\lambda_{t},\quad\text{ where }t=m\gamma_{1}.

For the left hand side, on the event
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
, since
a^{*}\leq 1/16
due to (
35
) and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(1)}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}^{(2)}=2^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{d\times d}
, we have

\displaystyle 5/16\leq(\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],k}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{[t],k}^{(k)}/t\leq 11/16,\quad\text{ for }k\in[2].

For the right-hand side, on the event
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}}
, due to the definition of
\lambda_{t}
in (
71
), we have that

\displaystyle-\frac{3\lambda_{t}}{2}\leq{(\boldsymbol{X}_{[k],k}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{[t]}}/{t}-\lambda_{t}\leq-\frac{\lambda_{t}}{2},\quad\text{ for }k\in[2]\text{ and }t=m\gamma_{1}.

Thus, on the event
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{all}}
, we have that for
t=m\gamma_{1}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}-\frac{24}{5}\lambda_{t}\leq\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t,1}^{(1)}-1\leq-\frac{8}{11}\lambda_{t},\quad\text{ and }\quad-\frac{24}{5}\lambda_{t}\leq\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{t,2}^{(2)}-\frac{1}{12}\leq-\frac{8}{11}\lambda_{t}.\end{split}

(81)

Step 2.
Lower bound the instantaneous regret.
In this step, let
m\in\{1,\ldots,\lfloor T/\gamma_{1}\rfloor\}
. By arguments similar to those in the proof of Theorem
6
(see Section
3.5
), for each
t\in(m\gamma_{1},(m+1)\gamma_{1}]
, we have

\displaystyle r_{t}=\left|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t}\right|\mathbbm{1}\{\textup{sgn}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\neq\textup{sgn}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{X}_{t})\}

where
\textup{sgn}(\tau):=\mathbbm{1}\{\tau>0\}-\mathbbm{1}\{\tau<0\}
for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
, and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}:=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)},\quad\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m}:=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(1)}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1}}^{(2)}.

Due to (
80
), we have that
\boldsymbol{u}_{j}=\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m,j}=0
for
j>2
, and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{[2]}=(1,-1/12)^{\prime},\quad\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m,[2]}=(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1},1}^{(1)},-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1},2}^{(2)})^{\prime}.

Due to Assumption
4 (a)
and since
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=\mathbb{I}_{d\times d}
, applying Lemma
33
to
\boldsymbol{X}_{t}
conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{m\gamma_{1}}
, there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that that for each
t\in(m\gamma_{1},(m+1)\gamma_{1}]
, almost surely (a.s.),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[r_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{m\gamma_{1}}\right]\geq C^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{[2]}\|_{2}\left\|\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{[2]}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}_{[2]}\|_{2}}-\frac{\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m,[2]}}{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m,[2]}\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2},

where
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m}/{\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m}\|_{2}}
is interpreted as zero if the denominator is zero.

Now, if

\displaystyle m\geq\tilde{\gamma}:=\max\left\{M_{*}s_{0}^{4},\left(\frac{48m_{X}}{c_{*}}\right)^{2}\right\},

(82)

then
\lambda_{m\gamma_{1}}\leq c^{*}
. By the definition of
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(1)},\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(2)}
, and due to (
80
), for each
t\in(m\gamma_{1},(m+1)\gamma_{1}]
, a.s.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[r_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{m\gamma_{1}}\right]\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{all}}\}\geq C^{-1}\frac{\sqrt{145}}{12}f\left(1-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1},1}^{(1)},\;1/12-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{m\gamma_{1},2}^{(2)}\right)\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{all}}\},

where
f
is defined in (
78
) and
C
is an absolute constant. Since
\lambda_{m\gamma_{1}}\leq c^{*}
and (
81
) holds, by Lemma
32
, we have that for each
t\in(m\gamma_{1},(m+1)\gamma_{1}]
, a.s.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[r_{t}|\mathcal{F}_{m\gamma_{1}}\right]\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{all}}\}\geq C^{-1}\frac{\sqrt{145}}{12}c_{*}\lambda_{m\gamma_{1}}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{m}^{\textup{all}}\},

(83)

which, due to (
79
), implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[r_{t}\right]\geq 0.4\frac{\sqrt{145}}{12}C^{-1}c_{*}\lambda_{m\gamma_{1}}^{2}.

Step 3.
Lower bound the cumulative regret.
Due to the above inequality, if
T\geq 2\tilde{\gamma}\gamma_{1}
where
\tilde{\gamma}
is defined in (
82
), then

\displaystyle\sum_{t=\tilde{\gamma}\gamma_{1}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[r_{t}\right]=\sum_{m=\tilde{\gamma}}^{\lfloor T/\gamma_{1}\rfloor}\sum_{t=m\gamma_{1}+1}^{(m+1)\gamma_{1}\wedge T}\mathbb{E}\left[r_{t}\right]\geq C^{-1}\sum_{m=\tilde{\gamma}}^{\lfloor T/\gamma_{1}\rfloor-1}\gamma_{1}\lambda_{m\gamma_{1}}^{2},

where
C>0
is an absolute constant. Then by the definition of
\lambda_{t}
in (
71
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=\tilde{\gamma}\gamma_{1}}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[r_{t}\right]\geq C^{-1}\sigma^{2}\sum_{m=\tilde{\gamma}}^{\lfloor T/\gamma_{1}\rfloor-1}\frac{\log(dT)}{m}\geq C^{-1}\sigma^{2}\log\left(\frac{\lfloor T/\gamma_{1}\rfloor}{\tilde{\gamma}+1}\right)(\log(d)+\log(T)),

where
C>0
is a constant that only depends on
m_{X}
. To summarize, there exists some constant
C^{*}>0
that only depends on
m_{X}
, such that if

C_{\gamma_{1}}\geq C^{*},\quad\text{ and }\quad T\geq(2C_{\gamma_{1}}C^{*}s_{0}^{5}\log(dT))^{\kappa},

then

\displaystyle\sum_{t=C^{*}s_{0}^{5}\log(dT)}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[r_{t}\right]\geq C^{-1}\sigma^{2}(1-1/\kappa)\log\left(T\right)(\log(d)+\log(T)).

∎

F
Auxiliary Results on Log-concave Densities

This appendix collects several results concerning log-concave densities. Let
\boldsymbol{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a random vector with a log-concave density
f_{\boldsymbol{Z}}
. Assume that
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{Z}]=0
, and let
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}:=\textup{Cov}(\boldsymbol{Z})=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\prime}]
.
Further, assume that for some constant
L>1
,

\displaystyle L^{-1}\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\right)\leq L.

(84)

For
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
, define
\text{sgn}(\tau):=\mathbbm{1}\{\tau>0\}-\mathbbm{1}\{\tau<0\}
.

Lemma 33
.

Assume (
84
) holds. Let
\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be two vectors. Assume
\boldsymbol{u}\neq\boldsymbol{0}_{d}
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
L
, such that

\mathbb{E}\left[{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z}|}\mathbbm{1}\{\textup{sgn}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})\neq\textup{sgn}(\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})\}\right]\leq\frac{C\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}.

If, in addition,
\boldsymbol{v}\neq\boldsymbol{0}_{d}
, then

C^{-1}{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}\left\|\frac{\boldsymbol{u}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z}|}\mathbbm{1}\{\textup{sgn}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})\neq\textup{sgn}(\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})\}\right].

Note that
\mathbb{E}\left[{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z}|}\right]\leq\sqrt{\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{Z}}\boldsymbol{u}}\leq\sqrt{L}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|
. Thus, if
\boldsymbol{v}=\boldsymbol{0}_{d}
, the upper bounds holds with
C=\sqrt{L}
.
Now, we focus on the case that
\boldsymbol{v}\neq\boldsymbol{0}_{d}
. By applying Lemma 3 in
[
36
]
with
\boldsymbol{u}/\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}
and
\boldsymbol{v}/\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}
, we have

C^{-1}\left\|\frac{\boldsymbol{u}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z}|}{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}\mathbbm{1}\{\textup{sgn}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})\neq\textup{sgn}(\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})\}\right]\leq C\left\|\frac{\boldsymbol{u}}{\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{v}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

The lower bound follows immediately. Moreover, together with Lemma 36 in
[
36
]
, the upper bound also holds.
∎

Lemma 34
.

Assume (
84
) holds. Let
m\geq 1
be an integer, and
\{\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\cdots\boldsymbol{u}_{m}\}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be an arbitrary set of orthonormal vectors (i.e.

\|\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\|_{2}=1
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{i}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{u}_{j}=0
for all
i\neq j\in[m]
), and denote by
f_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{u}_{m}}
the joint density of
(\boldsymbol{u}_{1}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z},\cdots,\boldsymbol{u}_{m}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
m,L
, such that
for all
\max_{i\in[m]}|\tau_{i}|\leq C^{-1}
, we have

f_{\boldsymbol{u}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{u}_{m}}(\tau_{1},\cdots,\tau_{m})\geq C^{-1}
.

The proof is essentially the same as that for Lemma 34(iii) in
[
36
]
, and thus omitted.
∎

Remark 8
.

We emphasize that the constant
C
in Lemma
34
does not depend on
d
, but only on
m
and
L
.

Lemma 35
(Margin Condition).

Assume (
84
) holds. Then there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
L
, such that for any non-zero
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{\boldsymbol{0}_{d}\}
, we have

\mathbb{P}(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z}|\leq\tau)\leq C\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}^{-1}\tau,\text{ for all }\tau>0.

It is due to Lemma 34(i) in
[
36
]
.
∎

Lemma 36
.

Assume (
84
) holds. Then for any constants
h,\ell_{2}>0
, there exists a constant
\ell_{1}>0
depending only on
\ell_{2},h,L
, such that

\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\left\{|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z}|\geq h\right\}]\right)\leq\ell_{2},\;\text{ for all }\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ such that }\|u\|_{2}\leq\ell_{1}.

The proof is essentially the same as the arguments in Appendix D.1 of
[
36
]
, combined with Lemma 34(ii) therein. Thus, it is omitted.
∎

For each arm
k\in[K]
and
h>0
, recall in (
27
) that
\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(k)}=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(k)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{x}>\max_{j\neq k}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{x}+h\}
, whose definition only involves the arm parameters.

Lemma 37
.

Suppose that (
84
) holds, and that Assumption
5
holds. Then there exist constants
h>0
and
\ell_{0}>0
depending only on
L,K,m_{\theta},L_{4}
, such that for each arm
k\in[K]
,

\lambda_{\min}\left(\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{Z}^{\prime}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{Z}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(k)}\}]\right)\geq\ell_{0}.

Fix an arbitrary vector
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}=1
. Without loss of generality, we consider the case
k=K
, and show that for some
h,\ell_{0}>0
, we have
\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{Z}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(K)}\}]\geq\ell_{0}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{i,j}=\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(i)}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(j)}
for
i,j\in[K]
. Then by definition,

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(K)}=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:(\boldsymbol{u}_{K,j})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{x}>h,\text{ for }j=1,\ldots,K-1\}.

We apply the Gram–Schmidt process to transform
\{\boldsymbol{u}_{K,1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{u}_{K,K-1},\boldsymbol{v}\}
into a set of
orthonormal
vectors
\{\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{K-1},\omega\}
. Specifically,
we set
\gamma_{1}:=\|\boldsymbol{u}_{K,1}\|_{2}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{1}=\boldsymbol{u}_{K,1}/\gamma_{1}
. Further, for
j\in[2,K-1]
, define

\displaystyle\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{j}:=\frac{\boldsymbol{u}_{K,j}-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{K,j}^{\prime}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i})\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}}{\gamma_{j,j}},\;\;\text{ where }\gamma_{j,j}:=\|\boldsymbol{u}_{K,j}-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}(\boldsymbol{u}_{K,j}^{\prime}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i})\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}\|_{2}

Due to Assumption
5 (b)
, this is well defined as
\gamma_{j,j}\geq L_{4}^{-1}
for
1\leq j\leq K-1
. Finally, if
\boldsymbol{v}
is in the linear span of
\{\boldsymbol{u}_{K,1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{u}_{K,K-1}\}
, let
\gamma_{K,K}=0
and
\boldsymbol{\omega}
be any unit vector such that
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\prime}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{j}^{(k)}=0
for all
j=1,\cdots,K-1
; otherwise, define

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\omega}:=\frac{\boldsymbol{v}-\sum_{i=1}^{K-1}(\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i})\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}}{\gamma_{K,K}},\;\;\text{ where }\gamma_{K,K}:=\|\boldsymbol{v}-\sum_{i=1}^{K-1}(\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i})\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}\|_{2}

Then by definition, we have that for
1\leq j\leq K-1
,

\boldsymbol{u}_{K,j}=\gamma_{j,j}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{j}+\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\gamma_{j,i}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i},\quad\boldsymbol{v}=\gamma_{K,K}\boldsymbol{\omega}+\sum_{i=1}^{K-1}\gamma_{K,i}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}

where
\gamma_{j,i}:=\boldsymbol{u}_{K,j}^{\prime}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}
and
\gamma_{K,i}=\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{i}
. Due to Assumption
5
and the subsequent remark,

\displaystyle\gamma_{j,j}\geq L_{4}^{-1},\quad|\gamma_{j,i}|\leq m_{\theta},\;\text{ for }1\leq i<j\leq K-1,\quad\sum_{i=1}^{K}\gamma_{K,i}^{2}=1.

(85)

Now, define
(\boldsymbol{W}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{W}_{K}):=(\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{1}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z},\ldots,\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{K-1}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z},\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})
, and then by definition

\displaystyle\{\boldsymbol{Z}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(K)}\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{j=1}^{K-1}\left\{\boldsymbol{W}_{j}>\gamma_{j,j}^{-1}(h-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\gamma_{j,i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle\supseteq\bigcap_{j=1}^{K-1}\left\{\boldsymbol{W}_{j}>L_{4}(h+\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}m_{\theta}|\boldsymbol{W}_{i}|)\right\}.

By elementary induction, we then have
\{(\boldsymbol{W}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{W}_{K-1})\in\mathcal{A}\}\subseteq\{\boldsymbol{Z}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(K)}\}
, where
\mathcal{A}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\left\{(\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{K-1})\in\mathbb{R}^{K-1}:(4m_{\theta}L_{4})^{i-1}L_{4}h\leq\tau_{i}\leq 2(4m_{\theta}L_{4})^{i-1}L_{4}h,\;\;\text{ for }i\in[K-1]\right\}.

As a result, due to the expression of
\boldsymbol{v}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{Z}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(K)}\}\right]\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{K}\gamma_{K,i}\boldsymbol{W}_{i}\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{(\boldsymbol{W}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{W}_{K-1})\in\mathcal{A}\}\right].

Finally, denote by
f
the joint density of
(\boldsymbol{W}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{W}_{K})^{\prime}
. By Lemma
34
, there exists a constant
C_{0}>0
depending only on
K,L
, such that

f(\tau_{1},\cdots,\tau_{K})\geq C_{0}^{-1}

for all
(\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{K})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
such that
\max_{i\in[K]}|\tau_{i}|\leq C_{0}^{-1}
.
Due to the definition of
\mathcal{A}
, if
h\leq 1/\left((4m_{\theta}L_{4})^{K}\right)
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{Z}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(K)}\}\right]\geq C_{0}^{-1}\int_{\mathcal{A}\times[-C_{0}^{-1},C_{0}^{-1}]}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{K}\gamma_{K,i}\tau_{i}\right)^{2}d\tau_{1}\ldots d\tau_{K}.

Denote by
r
the Lebesgure area of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
, where
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{A}\times[-C_{0}^{-1},C_{0}^{-1}]
. Further, let
(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{K})
be a random vector uniformly distributed on
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
. By definition,
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{K}
are independent, and then due to (
85
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z})^{2}\mathbbm{1}\{\boldsymbol{Z}\in\mathcal{U}_{h}^{(K)}\}\right]\geq rC_{0}^{-1}\text{Var}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{K}\gamma_{K,i}\xi_{i}\right)\geq rC_{0}^{-1}\min_{1\leq i\leq K}\text{Var}(\xi_{i}).

Then the proof is complete by letting

\ell_{0}:=rC_{0}^{-1}\min\{L_{4}^{2}h^{2}/12,\;1/(48C_{0}^{2})\}
.
∎

G
Some Fundamental Results

Lemma 38
.

Let
\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be two symmetric matrices, and
S\subseteq[d]
with
s:=|S|>0
. Assume that there exist
L,C>0
such that

L^{-1}\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\leq L
and
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\boldsymbol{B}-\boldsymbol{A}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\textup{max}}\leq C
.
Then

L^{-1}-C\times s\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{B}_{S,S}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{B}_{S,S}\right)\leq L+C\times s.

Let
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{s}
such that
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}=1
. By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,

\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}[(\boldsymbol{B}-\boldsymbol{A})_{S,S}]\boldsymbol{v}\right|\leq|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\boldsymbol{B}-\boldsymbol{A}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\textup{max}}\sum_{i\in S}\sum_{j\in S}|\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\boldsymbol{v}_{j}|\leq C(s\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2})=C\times s.

Due to the assumption,
L^{-1}\leq\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{A}_{S,S}\boldsymbol{v}\leq L
, which implies that
L^{-1}-C\times s\leq\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{B}_{S,S}\boldsymbol{v}\leq L+C\times s
