. The proof is then complete.
∎

Lemma 39
.

Let
\{B_{i}:i\in[n]\}
be a sequence of independent Bernoulli random variables. Assume that for some
\delta\in[0,1]
,
\mathbb{E}[B_{i}]\leq\delta
for
i\in[n]
. Then

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}B_{i}\geq 2\delta\right)\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{3}{8}n\delta\right\}.

By Bernstein’s inequality (Theorem 2.8.4 in
[
43
]
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(B_{i}-\delta\right)\geq n\delta\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(B_{i}-\mathbb{E}[B_{i}])\geq n\delta\right)\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{(n\delta)^{2}/2}{n\delta+(n\delta)/3}\right\}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 40
.

Let
\{\boldsymbol{A}_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}:\ell\in[N]\}
be a sequence of independent random matrices. Assume that for a constant
L>0
,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\boldsymbol{A}_{\ell}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}\leq L
for all
\ell\in[N]
. Then, for any
\epsilon>0
we have

\mathbb{P}\left({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}(\boldsymbol{A}_{\ell}-\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{A}_{\ell}])\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\textup{max}}\geq\epsilon\right)\leq 2mn\exp\left\{-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2L^{2}N}\right\}.

Fix
i\in[m]
and
j\in[n]
.
By the Hoeffding bound (see Proposition 2.5 in
[
45
]
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}((\boldsymbol{A}_{\ell})_{i,j}-\mathbb{E}[(\boldsymbol{A}_{\ell})_{i,j}])\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\leq 2\exp\left\{-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2L^{2}N}\right\}.

The proof is then complete by the union bound.
∎

G.1
Restricted Eigenvalues

Let
\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
be a matrix, where
d\geq 2
. Following Definition 1 in
[
33
]
, for
1\leq s\leq d
and
\kappa>0
, we define

\frac{1}{K(s,\kappa,\boldsymbol{A})}:=\min_{J\subseteq[d],|J|\leq s}\min_{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{J^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\kappa\|\boldsymbol{v}_{J}\|_{1}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{A}v\|_{2}}{\|v_{J}\|_{2}},\;\;\text{ for all }\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{\boldsymbol{0}_{d}\}.

(86)

This quantity is introduced to enable the application of results from
[
33
]
, and is closely related to the restricted eigenvalue condition in Definition
4
, as demonstrated in the following lemma.

Lemma 41
.

Let
a>0
and
\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
. Further, let
1\leq s\leq d
and
\kappa>0
.

i)

If

1/{K(s,\kappa,\boldsymbol{A}/\sqrt{n})}\geq a
,
then
\boldsymbol{A}
satisfies
\textup{RE}(s,\kappa,a^{2}/(1+\kappa))
.

ii)

If
\boldsymbol{A}
satisfies
\textup{RE}(s,\kappa,a)
, then
1/{K(s,\kappa,\boldsymbol{A}/\sqrt{n})}\geq\sqrt{a}
.

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{C}(s,k)
in Definition
4
. We begin with the first claim. Let
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{C}(s,k)
, and denote by
T_{0}
the locations of the
s
largest coefficients of
\boldsymbol{v}
in absolute values. Then by definition,

\|\boldsymbol{v}_{T_{0}^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\kappa\|\boldsymbol{v}_{T_{0}}\|_{1}
. Due to (
86
),

\displaystyle\frac{\|\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{n\|\boldsymbol{v}_{T_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{[K(s,\kappa,\boldsymbol{A}/\sqrt{n})]^{2}}\geq a^{2}.

By Lemma 12 of
[
33
]
, we have

\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}\leq(1+\kappa)\|\boldsymbol{v}_{T_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}
, which implies that
n^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}\geq(a^{2}/(1+\kappa))\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}
. Thus,
\boldsymbol{A}
satisfies
\textup{RE}(s,\kappa,a^{2}/(1+\kappa))
.

Now, we focus on the second claim. Let
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{\boldsymbol{0}_{d}\}
, and
J\subseteq[d]
such that
|J|\leq s
and
\|\boldsymbol{v}_{J^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\kappa\|\boldsymbol{v}_{J}\|_{1}
. By definition,
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{C}(s,k)
. As a result,

\displaystyle a\leq\frac{\|\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{n\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq\frac{\|(\boldsymbol{A}/\sqrt{n})\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}_{J}\|_{2}^{2}}.

Since the above holds for any such pair
\boldsymbol{v},J
, we have
1/{K(s,\kappa,\boldsymbol{A}/\sqrt{n})}\geq\sqrt{a}
. The proof is complete.
∎

Recall the
\psi_{2}
norm defined before Assumption
4
, and
\mathcal{C}(s,\kappa)
in Definition
4
.

Lemma 42
.

Let
\boldsymbol{Z}=(\boldsymbol{Z}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Z}_{n})^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}

be a random matrix. Let
1\leq s\leq d
,
\kappa,\alpha_{Z}>0
and
0<a<b
. Assume that
\boldsymbol{Z}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{Z}_{n}
are i.i.d.
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-random vectors, and that for each vector
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}=1
,
\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z}_{1}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\alpha_{Z}
. Further, let

\boldsymbol{A}:=\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{Z}_{1}\boldsymbol{Z}_{1}^{\prime}]
, and assume that

a\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\leq b
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0
depending only on
\alpha_{Z},\kappa,a,b
, such that for
n\geq Cs\log d
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-n/C}
,

\frac{a}{4}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\boldsymbol{v}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{Z}_{i}\boldsymbol{Z}_{i}^{\prime}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\leq\frac{9b}{4}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2},\quad\text{for all }\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{C}(s,\kappa).

Since
a\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)
, by Definition
4
,

n^{1/2}\boldsymbol{A}^{1/2}
satisfies
\textup{RE}(s,3\kappa,a)
for any
s\geq 1,\kappa>0
. By Lemma
41
,
1/K(s,3\kappa,A^{1/2})\geq\sqrt{a}
, where
K(\cdot)
is defined in (
86
).

Now, define
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{i}:=\boldsymbol{A}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{Z}_{i}
for
i\in[n]
, and
\boldsymbol{\Psi}=(\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{n})^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{Z}\boldsymbol{A}^{-1/2}
. Then,
\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\Psi}\boldsymbol{A}^{1/2}
. First, since
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}^{\prime}]
is the
d
-by-
d
identity matrix,
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}
is isotropic as defined in Definition 5 of
[
33
]
. Second, for any vector
\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}=1
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}\|_{\psi_{2}}=\|(\boldsymbol{A}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{Z}_{1}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{A}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{2}\alpha_{Z}.

Since
a\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)
, we have
\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq a^{-1/2}\alpha_{Z}
.

Finally, we apply Theorem 15 in
[
33
]
with
\delta=1/2
and
p=q=d
to obtain that there exists some constant
C>0
depending only on
\alpha_{Z},\kappa,a,b
such that if
n\geq Cs\log(d)
, with probability at least
1-2e^{-n/C}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\leq\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\Psi}\boldsymbol{A}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}}{n\|\boldsymbol{A}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}}\leq\frac{9}{4}.

Since

a\leq\lambda_{\min}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\boldsymbol{A}\right)\leq b
, we have
a\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{A}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}\leq b\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{2}^{2}
. The proof is then complete since
\boldsymbol{Z}=\boldsymbol{\Psi}\boldsymbol{A}^{1/2}
.
∎

Additional Simulation Results

Sequential Estimation.

We present additional simulation results for the sequential estimation setting, focusing on scenarios (a), (b), (c), and (d) as described in Section
2.4
.
Figure
6
displays the running cumulative estimation error from time
T/10
to time
t\in[T/10,T]
, comparing the performance of OPT-Lasso, Lasso, and an oracle estimator that has access to the true support and applies ordinary least squares on it.
Two different combinations of the tuning parameters
C_{0}
and
C_{0}^{\textup{hard}}
are considered.
Table
4
reports a sensitivity analysis showing the cumulative estimation error from time
T/10
to time
T
across these parameter combinations.

We present additional simulation results for the bandit setup, focusing on scenarios
(a), (b), (c), (d), (g) and (h) in Section
3.4
. In Figure
7
, we report the cumulative regret of the three-stage algorithm and the “Two-stage with Lasso” algorithm up to time
t
for
t\in(\gamma_{2},T]
.
In Table
5
, we present a sensitivity analysis of the three-stage algorithm to the tuning parameters
C_{0}
and
C_{0}^{\textup{hard}}
.

.

, respectively.
